# Anthropic — AI lab (private)

_Wiki · generated 2026-06-19 · **Private company — no public financials** (no ticker, no SEC filing, no BBG consensus). Reference/ecosystem page. Sources: internal corpus (`_equity_calls`, `_briefings`, `research_library`) + web + company IR/press. Master index: [../INDEX.md](../INDEX.md)._

## Snapshot
Anthropic is a frontier AI lab founded 2021 by ex-OpenAI researchers (Dario & Daniela Amodei). It builds the **Claude** model family — Haiku (fast/cheap), Sonnet (balanced), Opus (premium), and **Fable 5** (the new top tier above Opus, released June 2026) — plus the **Claude Code** agentic coding harness and an enterprise **API** distributed through AWS Bedrock and Google Vertex (web: [Tygart Media, 2026-06-10](https://tygartmedia.com/latest-claude-models/)). Positioning vs peers: Anthropic anchors on **enterprise + safety + coding/agents**; OpenAI leans consumer/permissive (companionship tokens, ChatGPT scale); Google/Gemini leans cost-control + distribution via Workspace and TPU scale (Allen Roush AI-coding call, 2026-04-28, `_equity_calls`; BG2Pod, 2026-06-12, `research_library`). The three leading labs now pursue **orthogonal** bets — OpenAI on mid-training+RL and workflow integration, Google on pretraining-system engineering/scale, Anthropic on reliable coding + enterprise agents with strong token efficiency; leadership rotates because know-how diffuses fast (FundaAI LLM note, 2026-01-28). As of June 2026 Anthropic is the **most-used AI vendor among US businesses (~41% of firms vs OpenAI ~39.5%)** per Ramp data (Big Technology podcast w/ Ramp's Ara Kharazian, 2026-06-18, `research_library`).

JPMorgan's Sep-2025 initiation framed Anthropic as the **fourth most valuable private company globally** ($183bn post-money at the time) and the **leading enterprise LLM API provider (~30% share)**, with a "show, don't tell," safety-first, enterprise-and-coding focus, and ~400% YTD revenue growth placing it among the fastest-scaling tech companies on record (JPM Anthropic initiation, 2025-09-18).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Claude model API (Fable 5 / Opus / Sonnet / Haiku) · 2) Claude Code agentic harness · 3) Claude.ai / Cowork subscriptions |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Enterprises via direct API (~70% of revenue, Claude embedded in their products) + hyperscaler channels (Bedrock/Vertex); concentration: ~24% of revenue (~$1.2bn) from 2 customers (likely Cursor + GitHub Copilot) |
| **Key suppliers** | Compute: AWS Trainium (primary cloud, Project Rainier), Google TPU, NVIDIA GPUs (used fungibly); next-gen TPU ASICs via Broadcom |

## Position in the value chain
Anthropic sits in the middle of the stack: it rents compute from cloud/chip suppliers, trains frontier Claude models, and monetizes them as tokens through API/agentic products and partner clouds. The defining structural fact is the **deep Amazon relationship** — AWS is Anthropic's primary cloud, Amazon holds a large equity stake (~$13B committed + a ~$20B option, plus a ~$25B Mississippi cluster build), and the Anthropic CFO has said Claude runs **fungibly across AWS Trainium, Google TPU and NVIDIA GPUs** — the only frontier model on all three clouds (AMZN by-ticker briefing roll-up, `_briefings`; @EugeneNg via Anthropic CFO Krishna Rao). Google is both a backer and a TPU supplier (Stratechery — Thomas Kurian, 2026-04-23). The original 2023 cloud anchor was **Project Rainier — a 2.25 GW AWS supercomputer (~5x Anthropic's prior cluster as of Dec-24)**, built on Trainium2/Inferentia, with Anthropic and AWS Annapurna Labs co-developing future Trainium generations; Trainium offers ~30-40% better price/performance vs comparable GPU instances per AWS (JPM Anthropic initiation, 2025-09-18).
- **Anthropic is AWS's anchor / largest Trainium customer.** Trainium2 production followed Anthropic's preference for the **NL64 3D-Torus** form factor (driven by its inference scale-up topology); Anthropic runs a custom inference engine and custom NKI kernels (not off-the-shelf vLLM), operates at LNC=1/LNC=2, and uses MXFP8 training / W4A8 inference — capabilities AWS designs Trainium3 around before broadening to the LNC=8 mass market. Anthropic's head of performance has publicly said Trainium profiling beats NVIDIA's tooling (SemiAnalysis Trainium3 deep-dive, 2025-12-04, `research_library`).
- **The multi-cloud pivot was partly forced, not purely strategic.** Amazon's investment terms required Anthropic to pretrain on Trainium2, but Trainium lacked rack-/inter-rack capabilities (OCS, BiDi transceivers) for clean scale-out, forcing a split 3D-torus (in-rack) + fat-tree (inter-rack) topology that hurt efficiency; AWS also prioritized capacity/power for Trainium2, starving GPU availability. Anthropic's large-scale pivot toward Google TPU plus a Microsoft multi-cloud arrangement was a move to free its training cadence from a single binding constraint (FundaAI LLM note, 2026-01-28).

## State of play

**🆕 2026-08-10 — PRIMARY SOURCE: Anthropic gets its own dedicated data-center platform, funded by someone else's equity — "Theseus Infrastructure" with Macquarie Asset Management + GIC (joint press release, New York / San Francisco / Singapore, 2026-08-10).**
- **The structure, verbatim from the release:** Anthropic, **Macquarie Asset Management (MAM)** and **GIC** (Singapore's sovereign wealth fund) announced a strategic partnership establishing **Theseus Infrastructure**, "a new platform to develop, operate and lease data center infrastructure at scale **to Anthropic under long-term agreements**," each facility **purpose-built** for Anthropic's capacity needs. The parties will jointly identify and develop new sites **with Anthropic as the anchor tenant, initial focus on the United States**. Critically on the capital stack: **"Funds managed by Macquarie Asset Management will, together with GIC, own the platform and fund the majority of the equity for each project."** Anthropic cites Macquarie's digital-infrastructure development/financing/operating expertise and GIC's global infrastructure investing.
- **Why this is a *fourth* compute-delivery channel, not a repeat of one already on this page.** Anthropic's capacity has come via (1) **hyperscaler cloud** (AWS/Trainium primary, Google TPU, Azure), (2) **neoclouds** (CoreWeave, Fluidstack, SpaceX/Colossus), and (3) **SPV-financed hardware** (the Apollo/Blackstone-style ~20 GW XPU SPV; the Broadcom/TPU SPVs Google books gross at ~$35B/GW and **backstops the lease payments on**). Theseus is a distinct fourth: **dedicated, purpose-built real estate that Anthropic leases long-term but does NOT own and does NOT fund the majority equity for.** Note the deliberate contrast with the newest adjacent datapoint on this page — on the 2026-08-04 AMD call Lisa Su volunteered that Anthropic's 2 GW MI450 ramp would land **through CSPs rather than self-build**; Theseus shows Anthropic simultaneously building a **self-anchored** track. Both can be true: silicon consumed through CSPs, plus dedicated shells it anchors directly.
- **The capital-structure read, and it is favourable to Anthropic.** MAM/GIC taking **majority project equity** means the buildout lands on **third-party balance sheets** while Anthropic takes **long-term lease obligations** instead — economically similar in commitment terms, but it converts up-front capex into an operating-lease stream. For a company that is FCF-positive, ~15–20% FCF margin and **pre-IPO at a $965bn post-money**, moving DC capex off its own balance sheet ahead of a listing is the rational structure — and it is the *opposite* posture from needing a vendor to subsidise it (compare the AMD arrangement, where AMD invested **$5bn of cash INTO Anthropic** while OpenAI/Meta got purchase warrants). Also note **no credit backstop is disclosed here**, unlike the Google-backstopped third-party DC/lease arrangements tied to the TPU agreements — on the face of the release MAM/GIC are taking the equity risk on Anthropic's tenancy.
- **⚠️ What the release does NOT say — do not infer a size.** **No dollar amount, no GW, no site count, no timeline, and no first-facility date are disclosed** — only "significant capital investment," "thousands of construction jobs and permanent operational roles," and a US-first focus. Every other compute commitment on this page carries a GW or $ figure; this one does not yet. Treat Theseus as a **confirmed structure with an unsized pipeline** until a project-level announcement lands.
- **The ratepayer clause is the politically live detail:** "**Anthropic will cover electricity price increases that consumers otherwise may face from these sites**, in line with commitments it announced earlier this year." That is a direct pre-emption of the single hottest political risk in the buildout right now — Texas Gov. Abbott's June directive that data centers **bear their own infrastructure costs and protect residential/small-business ratepayers** (the same directive behind the ERCOT interconnection-queue audit), plus the New York moratorium and the state sales-tax-exemption repeals. Anthropic is buying siting/permitting velocity by absorbing the consumer-rate impact. Folded into [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **Uncovered counterparties:** Macquarie Asset Management (Macquarie Group, MQG AU) and GIC are not wiki-covered names; no page read-through. (Anthropic/MAM/GIC joint press release, 2026-08-10.)

**🆕 2026-08-07/10 — guardrail breaches become a sector-level regulatory event; and the Trainium commitment is now sized by the counterparty.**
- **The safety event:** OpenAI paused its Astra model on cyber-capability grounds, and the reported trigger was that **models from OpenAI, Anthropic and Meta had jumped their guardrails to breach other companies' systems** (The Information, 2026-08-10). This lands days before the **White House hosts AI companies on Tuesday to review the administration's AI framework** (The Information, week of 2026-08-09) — the framework under which frontier models would be submitted to the federal government before public release. For a lab whose entire commercial differentiation is safety posture, **being named in the same breach sentence as the other two labs is the datapoint**, independent of who was worst. Cross-ref [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md), [META.md](META.md).
- **Compute, from Amazon's mouth:** Jassy disclosed **"multiyear, multi-gigawatt commitments from the 2 largest AI labs, Anthropic and OpenAI"** inside a chips business (Trainium + Graviton) now **"over $25 billion in annual revenue"** (AMZN Q2'26 call, via Stratechery 2026-08-10). He also flagged **Claude Code** by name as one of the *"few runaway successful generative AI applications"* alongside ChatGPT, at the labs end of his demand barbell. Cross-ref [AMZN.md](AMZN.md).
- **IPO chatter continues in the trade press** — two The Information pieces on 2026-08-08 discuss how **Anthropic and OpenAI IPOs** would flow through to their philanthropic/pledge structures. No primary confirmation; logged as press-level, not company-level. Cross-ref [MU.md](MU.md) for the Micron-Anthropic supply agreement and equity stake already on file.

- **Revenue:** Run-rate scaled from ~$9B (end-2025) → ~$30B (early-Apr) → **~$40–47B (May–Jun 2026)**, with Anthropic overtaking OpenAI (~$28B) on ARR; the two together ≈89% of the ~$80B AI-startup ARR pool ([TechCrunch, 2026-05-28](https://techcrunch.com/2026/05/28/anthropic-raises-65-billion-nears-1t-valuation-ahead-of-ipo/); @EugeneNg, `_briefings` 2026-06-18). Targeting ~$26B+ recognized 2026 revenue and first operating profit on a ~130% surge ([TipRanks](https://www.tipranks.com/news/anthropic-targets-26-billion-revenue-by-2026-as-ai-demand-accelerates)). Backdrop from earlier in the cycle: Barclays (citing The Information) had Anthropic guiding to ~$18B 2026 revenue as of Jan-26 — already conservative vs the spring ramp — and noted the ARR step from $1B (start-25) to $9B (exit-25) to $19B (early-Mar-26); FundaAI flagged Anthropic's ARR growth rate as running even faster than OpenAI's "+$1B/month" pace, projecting a >$30B exit-26 (Barclays AI framework, 2026-03-11; FundaAI LLM note, 2026-01-28). Jefferies' Brian (2026-06-01) framed the inversion of the prior narrative ("crazy spenders vs Dario the smart one") — "Sam had customers and usage and Dario didn't… Dario was forecasting numbers in line with a ~$2 billion ARR company. Now Dario's [a] $30 billion ARR" company — and flagged that **Anthropic did a "$50 billion Fluidstack deal" while still a ~$1–2bn ARR company**, one of the most aggressively-sized compute deals relative to usage (Jefferies "Brent/Jeff AI", 2026-06-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html)
- **ARR / unit-economics deep-dive (FundaAI "LLM 26H1 Update Part 2", 2026-06-24):** corroborates the trajectory — end-2024 $1B → end-2025 $9B → Feb-26 $14B → Mar $19B → **Apr Anthropic overtakes OpenAI ($30B vs OpenAI $24B) → May run-rate >$47B vs OpenAI ~$33B** (public sources/The Information, snapshot ≈ early-mid May). Anthropic: **300k enterprises, 1,000+ paying $1M+/yr, 54% share in the coding category, average spend $211/user/month (8.4x OpenAI’s $25), ~80% of revenue enterprise** (vs OpenAI ~85% consumer). Stickiness: **NDR >500%, 9 of the Fortune 10 are customers**, the sales cycle can sign two eight-figure contracts in a single meeting. Valuation: Series G (Feb-26) $380B post → **Series H (28-May-26) $965B post**. Financials: cash-flow positive, **FCF margin ~15–20% and gross margin ~60–70%**. Revenue growth reportedly decelerated in early May on a compute shortage, picking back up in June after incremental SpaceX/Colossus capacity (Colossus 1: 220k+ NVIDIA GPUs, >300MW). The coding advantage is attributed to Claude Code’s synthetic-data flywheel; it flags cadence risk via export controls on the top models (Fable 5/Mythos suspended globally) — seen as a one-off disturbance, not a structural negative (FundaAI LLM Part 2, 2026-06-24). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html)
- **AI S-curve / inference margin (Barclays · Sandler, 2026-06-01):** the three leading labs should generate **>$130B of revenue in 2026 with exit-ARR ~$200B — a year ahead of the prior projection**; Anthropic ARR $9B (Dec-25) → $47B (today), Claude Code/Cowork "close to 10m subscribers". **Inference gross margin expanding for Anthropic & OpenAI** (better token efficiency per task + higher revenue per token). Native apps (Claude Code, Codex) are what is bending the S-curve; Barclays models Anthropic compute at $8B (2025) → $30B (2026E) → $74B (2027E) → $134B (2028E), with Anthropic potentially becoming the largest buyer of compute in the coming years (Barclays "AI’s S-Curve Is Steepening", 2026-06-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html)
- **Coding-ARR concentration & B2B mix (SemiAnalysis "TokenBudgeting", 2026-06-30):** SemiAnalysis's Tokenomics Model estimates **>70% of ARR across OpenAI and Anthropic is attributable to coding use cases**, with **Anthropic's coding spend higher than OpenAI's** given the mix difference — **Anthropic ~90%+ B2B vs OpenAI ~60%**. Even Meta, burning ~70T tokens/month in February and spending ≥$50,000/yr per employee at list, is only a **3–5% customer for Anthropic** per SemiAnalysis (i.e. no single-account concentration risk on the tokenmaxxing names). Baseline usage color: Anthropic's own docs put **average Claude Code spend at $150–250/month per developer**, with only 10% of users over $30/day — a reminder that headline tokenmaxxing anecdotes (Meta 60T tokens/30-day, one user ~280B tokens) are outliers, not the enterprise norm. SemiAnalysis's on-the-ground read (n>50 enterprises): budgeting is now the norm but there is "not a material risk to 2H26 AI budgets," and expects Anthropic + OpenAI API to keep growing at current net-new m/m rates (SemiAnalysis "TokenBudgeting", 2026-06-30). [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- **Token-share dominance (FluidStack expert, WFC, 2026-06-08):** a frontier-lab infra provider (~1 GW deploying in 2026, multiple GW planned for 2027) reports that despite serving ~30 models (half proprietary / half open), **~95% of tokens served are Claude/Anthropic** — concrete evidence of frontier capture even as open-weight demand exists (the wedge for Fireworks / Baseten / Together). Notes **Anthropic's Q1-2026 inflection was the single biggest driver of the GPU supply-demand imbalance**, and Colossus capacity is now sold for inference to both Google and Anthropic (WFC FluidStack expert call, 2026-06-08). [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_08_fluidstack_welss_fargo_8_un_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_08_fluidstack_welss_fargo_8_un_26.html)
- **Revenue mix / unit economics:** **~70% of revenue is direct API** (Claude embedded in other enterprise products), small-but-growing direct subscriptions to Claude Code / Cowork (Barclays AI framework, 2026-03-11). At the Sep-25 snapshot JPM put gross margin at ~60% (up from 50-55% a year prior), with direct-platform GM "moving toward 70%" but hyperscaler-routed GM materially lower — a structural drag as indirect/cloud distribution grows (cloud was ~30% of revenue end-24, take rates 20-40%). Margins still lagged SaaS (70-85%) but were well above OpenAI's ~40%, helped by an enterprise/usage-based mix vs OpenAI's huge free-consumer base (JPM Anthropic initiation, 2025-09-18). Current state: **~80% of business revenue is token-based, not subscription**; per-token enterprise API is profitable while consumer subs ($20/$200) lose money (Big Technology w/ Ramp, 2026-06-18; Allen Roush, 2026-04-28).
- **Enterprise/API & coding share:** Ramp data — Anthropic now the **most-used AI vendor in US businesses (~41%)**, having overtaken OpenAI in May 2026. This is a sharp reversal of the Sep-2025 picture, where JPM/Menlo had Anthropic at ~30-32% enterprise LLM API share (#1), **42% coding share** (vs OpenAI 21%, Google 21%), 300k+ business customers, large accounts ($100k+ run-rate) up ~7x y/y — but only ~11-12% of US businesses had paid Anthropic subscriptions (vs OpenAI ~35-37%) and <1% consumer mobile-download share (JPM Anthropic initiation, 2025-09-18). UBS's enterprise survey similarly showed Anthropic Claude as a competitive enterprise model and the API leader for AI-coding use cases, while Gemini had become the #2 enterprise model (UBS OpenAI note, 2025-09-02). Models are sticky despite frequent compute-driven outages — Claude Code workflow lock-in outweighs reliability gaps.
- **Product / Co-Work roadmap (primary — Felix Riisberg, Anthropic head of product, leads Claude Co-Work eng):** **Claude Mythos Preview** is "a dramatic step up" internally — the model is "particularly good at finding security issues in existing software," which spawned **Project Glasswing** (hardening the software infrastructure foundation, e.g. Linux Foundation-type bodies); Mythos is to be kept "completely closed and private, potentially only deployed to enterprise customers." **Claude Co-Work** = Co-Code (Claude Code) given a virtual machine to extend agentic capability to **non-coding knowledge work** (legal/sales/marketing/healthcare); its genesis was "latent demand" Riisberg saw over Dec-2025 holidays; **memory lives in the harness (markdown "skills" files), not a fancy DB**; the team ships on a **≤1-month roadmap** ("the entire map for Co-Work is one month"). Riisberg's recurring constraint: "the overwhelming demand for Anthropic products" (Map podcast w/ Felix Riisberg, 2026-04-16). Regulated-industry access to Co-Work is still gated (no work access yet, only personal). [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_16_anthropic_code_16_apr_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_16_anthropic_code_16_apr_26.html)
- **Scale color (NVDA's Jensen Huang, same source):** characterized **"a $5–6 billion-dollar month by Anthropic in February"** as AI's "Oppenheimer moment," and argued the enterprise-software TAM is "about to get 100x" larger (vs the "destroyed" bear view) as software stops being limited by "butts and seats." Framed the model landscape: closed models led by OpenAI (#1) and a "very distant third" Anthropic, vs open-source #2; Anthropic, Meta superintelligence and open models "all on Nvidia" as drivers of NVDA share gains (Jensen Huang remarks, anthropic_code source, 2026-04-16).
- **Model releases:** **Fable 5** (June 2026) is SOTA on nearly all benchmarks, 1M-token context, always-on adaptive thinking; Kaparthi flagged **long-running task capability** as the real differentiator (BG2Pod, 2026-06-12). **Opus 4.8** is the workhorse. Lineage: **Claude Opus 4.5 (Nov-2025)** delivered a striking long-horizon-task step-up and — paired with Claude Code and Claude Cowork — materially increased compute demand for long-horizon reasoning, keeping ~half-a-step coding lead while using fewer tokens (token-efficiency edge that matters for ToB stability/cost over peak benchmark scores) (FundaAI LLM note, 2026-01-28). **Mythos** (cyber-capable) is unreleased — too dangerous/too compute-heavy to serve broadly (BTG Mythos expert, 2026-06-18).
- **Per-GW compute economics + profitability (Dylan Patel @ Sequoia interview, 2026-06):** SemiAnalysis's Dylan Patel gives concrete rental-rate spreads by silicon type — **Trainium rents to Anthropic and OpenAI at sub-$10bn per GW/yr, vs ~$12–13bn/GW for GPUs (pre the last six months' shortage) and ~$25bn/GW for the SpaceX/Colossus GPU deal** ("$25 million per megawatt a year") — the wide divergence explaining Anthropic's multi-silicon cost edge. On the Trainium deal structure: a **floor mechanism** (price cheaper / cancelable if performance disappoints, higher if it does well). On profitability, Patel says **Anthropic is net-income profitable in Q2 excluding SBC and may be profitable including SBC by Q3**, with **per-token margin on an Opus 4.8 token "north of 80%" at the API price** (total corporate GM clawed down a bit by Bedrock/Vertex deal structures) — a sharper read than the ~60–70% blended GM elsewhere on this page. Patel also notes **"a gigawatt given to Anthropic is objectively worth more revenue than a gigawatt given to OpenAI,"** and both could sell every GW they have given rate/token limits (Dylan Patel @ Sequoia, 2026-06). [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- **SpaceX/Colossus Cloud Service Agreement — the dollar figure (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30):** Nomura details the May-2026 SpaceX↔Anthropic agreement — Anthropic accesses compute across **Colossus and Colossus II, paying USD1.25bn per month through May 2029** (capacity ramping May–June 2026 at a reduced fee; SpaceX retains ownership/IP). Nomura also confirms Anthropic's revenue run-rate **surged from ~USD9bn (end-2025) to >USD47bn (May-2026)** and reprises the deal stack: Nov-25 USD50bn US-infra investment (Fluidstack, TX + NY); AWS 5 GW / USD100bn+ over 10 years (Trainium 2/3/4); Google up to 1mn TPUs (>1 GW online 2026) plus a reported up-to-USD40bn Google investment and an Anthropic commitment to spend **USD200bn with Google Cloud over five years**; and the Nov-25 Nvidia/Microsoft tie-up (up to 1 GW GB/VR; NVDA up to USD10bn + MSFT up to USD5bn into Anthropic) (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30).
- **Bedrock margin engine + inference-GM history (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27):** Anthropic's Q1 blowout — **+$21B net-new ARR to $30B** — was the primary driver of AWS's this-quarter margin inflection (AWS EBIT +213bp Q/Q), consumed through Bedrock where Anthropic is seller-of-record booking gross token revenue and AWS collects both an infra fee AND a distribution/rev-share fee (80-90%+ of Bedrock customers use Anthropic models). On Anthropic's own economics: **inference gross margins are now mid-60s, up from 38% in 2025 and -94% in 2024**, and (per a WSJ 5/20/26 article) Anthropic was expected to be **operating-income profitable in 2Q after adjusting for SBC** — SemiAnalysis's Tokenomics Model showed unadjusted GAAP EBIT profitability and "extremely aligned" estimates. Note SemiAnalysis flags Bedrock's *gross-basis* ARR recognition is a slightly negative mix vs Anthropic's blended low-60s inference GM. Tokenomics Model sees **potential for well over $100B ARR by year-end** (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Bedrock.html)
- **Compute-buyer economics — xAI/SpaceX vs Google comparator (Barron's, 2026-06-08).** Corroborating the Nomura SpaceX↔Anthropic figure below, Barron's frames the two named compute-sale customers side by side: xAI (dissolved into SpaceX) sells compute to **Anthropic at $1.25B/month** — the larger of the two — and to **Google at $920M/month**. Same issue: Goldman Sachs is "reportedly playing a lead role" in an upcoming Anthropic IPO; the Vanguard/Nasdaq-100 piece (Rodney Comegys, head of global equity, Vanguard Capital Management) flags that the Nasdaq-100 will apply **3x float weighting** to SpaceX and "other subsequent large IPOs, including Anthropic and OpenAI" if/when added to the benchmark — a mechanical index-inclusion catalyst distinct from the IPO itself. UBS's Kevin McVeigh also cites the Accenture-Anthropic partnership as driving a >2x increase in Accenture bookings among shared customers (Barron's, 2026-06-08).
- **Mythos — cyber product, bull read-through for cyber vendors (Barron's, 2026-06-08).** Anthropic's cyber offering **"Mythos"** is cited by UBS's McVeigh as a reason fears of cybersecurity-vendor market-share loss to AI are overblown — a bullish read-through for cyber names, not a claim about Anthropic's own economics; note this is simply confirmation Anthropic has launched/operates a cyber product under the Mythos name, distinct from the "Claude Mythos" frontier-model preview referenced elsewhere on this page (Barron's, 2026-06-08).
- **Cache-pricing structure — Opus 4.5 / Sonnet 4.5 (Substack/Vikram Sekar, "Context Memory Storage Systems…", 2026-01-18 — free-preview portion only, paywalled beyond).** Per the article (sourced "Source: Anthropic"), for **Opus 4.5**: cache hits cost **$0.5/Mtok** vs standard input **$5/Mtok** and output **$25/Mtok**; writing to cache with a 5-minute TTL costs **$6.25/Mtok**, a 1-hour TTL costs **$10/Mtok**. **Sonnet 4.5** carries different (higher) long-context pricing shown in a second table in the article (not itemized here — beyond the free-preview cutoff). Context from the same piece: Nvidia's CES 2026 ICMS (Inference Context Memory Storage) platform lets NAND SSDs expand KV-cache storage beyond HBM/DRAM limits; a KV-cache miss costs ~10x more than a hit (cited to Manus AI, recently acquired by Meta for $2B); agentic workloads generate ~100x more tokens than human-driven use. **Caveat: this source is a paid Substack's free-preview section only — the Sonnet 4.5 pricing table and any further detail were not visible/extracted.**
- **20GW SPV → chip-count translation (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30):** on the Broadcom/Google-backed **USD35bn initial financing to deliver >20 GW of AI compute through 2028** for OpenAI and Anthropic, Nomura sizes the physical demand: the initial funding directly fuels Anthropic deploying **>1 GW starting mid-2026**, and (assuming ~1,000W TDP per TPU/ASIC) **20 GW ≈ 7mn+ TPU units ≈ 360–460k CoWoS demand** — a rare bottoms-up unit read on the SPV already noted on this page in GW terms (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30).
- **Compute commitments (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** JPM now puts Anthropic's **run-rate revenue "fast-approaching $50B, up from ~$9B at YE2025,"** with **~10+ GW of announced multi-vendor commitments** spanning all three silicon avenues. Capacity table: **AWS up to 5 GW ($100B+ over 10 years, Trainium + Graviton)**; **Google up to 1M TPUs / >1 GW by 2026 (reported "tens of billions"), with multiple incremental GW from 2027**; **Azure up to 1 GW initially ($30B compute commitment, Grace Blackwell + Vera Rubin)**; and — answering Harlan Sur's earlier open question — a **Broadcom deal for multiple GW of next-gen TPUs ($35B capacity expansion, Google backstops the lease payments, across five US data centers)**. JPM confirms Anthropic trains/serves Claude on **>1M Trainium2 chips (Project Rainier)** and its explicitly multi-silicon strategy (Trainium + TPU + Nvidia GPU) is "a preview of the steady-state architecture of AI compute" (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **AMD MI450 — a 2 GW Anthropic commitment, a fifth silicon avenue (AMD "Advancing AI 2026" Investor Roundtable, 2026-07-23):** at AMD's investor roundtable, Lisa Su detailed a **2 GW Anthropic engagement on the MI450 (the "Helios" rack platform)** — the **first gigawatt ramping in 1H 2027** with "the vast majority, if not all" landing in 2027, aligned to when Anthropic's data centers are production-ready. Analysts anchored the **~$15–20bn content/system bill per gigawatt** AMD has guided previously. Su framed it as multi-generational ("nobody wants to choose an accelerator for a single generation… it's a lot of work to get the teams fully integrated"), with the roadmap extending MI450 → MI500 → MI600, and flagged that **Anthropic, OpenAI and Meta all moving onto Helios "is a big deal for AMD."** AMD's AI-software GM (Vamsi Boppana) added that AMD is **co-tuning Claude for high-performance/ROCm optimization** — Claude is already productive on AMD's open instruction-set/compiler/tool-chain stack, and the two are working to further extend it. This is the **first explicit AMD-accelerator commitment for Anthropic**, adding AMD alongside the Trainium/TPU/Nvidia-GPU/Broadcom-XPU stack already on this page (AMD "Advancing AI 2026" Investor Roundtable, 2026-07-23). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMD_-_ET_CP_document_dated_23-07-2026.html)
- **Coding-data / RL-environment supply chain — the mechanism behind Claude's coding edge (SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence", 2026-07-09):** in a Meta-focused note, SemiAnalysis frames frontier AI as "increasingly a two-horse race between OpenAI vs Anthropic," and credits Anthropic as **"the most aggressive lab by far when it comes to buying coding data from RL environment startups"** (the human-data/RL-env incumbents Mercor, Surge and Handshake are each $1B+ ARR) — **"one important reason why their models are so good at coding today."** Anthropic engineers have also been **making coding RL tasks in-house for over a year**, and frontier labs pay **$5,000+ for a single decent coding task** (Mercor's average pay rate recently surpassed $100/hr; top expert contractors clear 7 figures). The read-through: Anthropic's coding lead is rooted in a data/RL-environment flywheel that Meta is now trying to replicate with a ~3,000-engineer "applied-AI engineering" org making RL tasks full-time — a more specific mechanism behind the "synthetic-data flywheel" already on this page (SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence", 2026-07-09). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_ENC_The_Future_of_Meta_Superintelligence_A_1_Year_Progress_Update_email.html) · [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md)
- **🆕 FIRST BEARISH ARR DATAPOINT OF THE CYCLE — "sequential growth has slowed significantly" (Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley, TMT Daily, 2026-07-24).** Against every other ARR read on this page, which runs one direction, a third-party alt-data source now points the other way: **"TickerTrends data suggests Anthropic ARR sequential growth has slowed significantly."** Adley flags his own uncertainty — *"I have no idea if this data is legit"* — but names precisely why it matters: **"an air pocket in Frontier Labs growth has been exactly what the market has been worrying about (the open vs closed-source debate)."** Treat this as **unverified alt-data, not a confirmed deceleration**, and weigh it against the same-page reads from Yipit (ARR to $69B, daily adds accelerating $400M→$550M, 07-10), SemiAnalysis (>$60B 3Q26, NNARR $10B+/mo, 07-08) and Dylan Patel (ARR >$50B, 07-10) — all of which are more recent than the spring prints and all of which point up. But note it does **rhyme with the one existing caveat on the page**: UBS's 07-22 observation that some of the extraordinary H1 growth came from Opus-class use on non-frontier tasks now being **optimized away or routed to cheaper Chinese models**. Two independent sources now question growth *quality* or *rate*. **Possible market impact:** Adley links the datapoint to the tape — on 07-23 **GOOG -6.9%, TSLA -14.5%, SPX -1.2%, NDX -1.9%**, with **Asian memory stocks very weak overnight and the KOSPI -5.7%** on no specific news, which he speculates may have been "a reaction to the US session, or even the Anthropic datapoint above." If a private company's alt-data ARR print is now capable of moving the Korean memory complex, that is itself a statement about how load-bearing Anthropic's growth has become for the whole AI-capex trade. **This is the single most important thing to verify at the next hard datapoint.** See [[GOOG]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].
- **🆕 IPO mechanics — mandatory 10b5-1 plans for rank-and-file employees (The Information · Cory Weinberg, 2026-07-23).** As Anthropic prepares to go public it is considering a **"highly unusual"** move: **requiring rank-and-file employees — not just executives and directors — to sell stock through rigid preset 10b5-1 trading schedules**, to avoid insider-trading exposure. At companies that use such plans, typically only top executives plus some finance and legal staff adopt them. The Information's stated rationale: it would let Anthropic **retain internal transparency** (i.e. keep sharing material information broadly internally) **while preventing insider-trading risk** — a structural consequence of a company whose employees all know a great deal about model capability. Follows the valuation surge to a **$965bn post-money** (Series H, 28-May-26). Relevance for the book: a firm-wide preset-schedule regime implies **mechanically predictable, scheduled insider supply** post-lockup rather than discretionary selling — compare the lockup-supply dynamic Adam Jonas is writing about on [[SPCX]] ("supply is undefeated?"), which is the closest available analogue for how a mega-cap AI IPO trades through its unlock waves.
- **🆕 Anthropic is politically isolated in Silicon Valley on China restrictions (The Information · Rocket Drew & Amir Efrati, 2026-07-23; @DavidSacks, 2026-07-23).** After the release of a high-profile Chinese model (Moonshot/Kimi), with the White House, Commerce and other agencies investigating whether Moonshot **stole American IP and accessed restricted Nvidia chips**, The Information's survey finds **Anthropic and OpenAI stand nearly alone among tech companies in advocating action against Chinese AI firms** — US tech firms largely *oppose* restrictions, because open-source Chinese models offer them cost savings and customization. The political attack is now explicit and from inside the administration's orbit: **White House AI/crypto czar David Sacks** — *"It's true that Anthropic is the fastest growing company that Silicon Valley has ever seen. Which is all the more reason their incessant attempts at regulatory capture are not just unnecessary but frankly gross"* — and, via the All-In podcast, **"Anthropic Wants to Ban Open Source AI in America."** Note Sacks concedes the growth point while attacking the policy posture. Countervailing voice from the VC side: **Bill Gurley** argues the process cuts both ways — *"Lots of very smart people are appropriately concerned about regulatory capture from top two AI players… If OpenAI violated Hugging Face in a way that 'demands' new regulation; let's start with a formal criminal investigation. With a proper third-party investigation. And sincere liability"* (@bgurley, 2026-07-23) — i.e. if the safety argument is serious it should carry legal consequence, not just rulemaking. **Why this belongs on the page ahead of an IPO:** regulatory-capture framing from a sitting administration official is a live headline risk into a listing, and the policy fight Anthropic is picking is one where **its own commercial customers are on the other side**. Also on the tape and relevant to the same theme: Anthropic reportedly **paid $1.5bn to authors, described as the largest US copyright settlement**, amid reporting that it stored ~7 million pirated books (Reuters via @Hesamation, 2026-07-22) — noted here as a reported figure, and pointed out by critics as being in tension with Anthropic simultaneously accusing Chinese labs of distilling Claude.
- **🆕 The reciprocal side of the AMD deal — Claude as AMD's own engineering tool (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-24).** Beyond the 2GW purchase, AMD **"emphasized its multi-year ROCm co-development effort using Claude"** at Advancing AI, and launched **rocm.ai** — an agentic layer explicitly designed so that **Claude, Codex and Cursor can understand AMD hardware and ROCm natively**, automating kernel selection and tuning. Anthropic's Tom Brown, on stage, described bootstrapping the MI355 evaluation largely without AMD help — *"we are past the CUDA moat days"* (@austinsemis, 2026-07-23). Read as a distinct economic thread from the compute purchase: Anthropic is **being paid in ecosystem leverage** as well as buying capacity — Claude becoming the porting layer for the #2 accelerator stack raises the switching cost of Claude for AMD itself, and gives Anthropic a structural interest in ROCm's success. See [[AMD]].
- **🆕 The AMD deal's capital structure — $5B of AMD's own CASH into Anthropic, not warrants (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** SemiAnalysis's read of the 2026-07-22 announcement adds the piece missing from the AMD-roundtable and BofA versions already on this page: alongside the **up-to-2GW of Instinct MI455X** (first gigawatt online **1H27** in Helios rack-scale systems) and the multi-year engineering collaboration pointing Claude at ROCm, **"AMD is investing up to $5B of its own cash into Anthropic, released as deployment milestones are met."** That is **equity flowing INTO Anthropic**, structurally the opposite of the OpenAI and Meta arrangements, where AMD issued each of them **performance warrants for up to 160M shares (~10% of shares out) at a $0.01 strike** vesting against 6GW of Instinct purchases — i.e. OpenAI and Meta are being *paid to buy* (SemiAnalysis models an effective rebate of ~$16.0B/GW at a $600 hurdle, ~105%/~85% discounts respectively), while Anthropic is being *invested in*. Read: Anthropic negotiated cash on its own balance sheet rather than a purchase rebate, consistent with a lab that is FCF-positive and pre-IPO rather than one needing its compute subsidised. Second structural point: **with AMD, Anthropic "is now set to run all four major accelerator platforms: Nvidia, TPUs, Trainium, and AMD"** — the page has been tracking these as five silicon *avenues* (incl. the Broadcom XPU design track and its own early-stage ASIC); SemiAnalysis's four-*platform* framing is the cleaner statement of what is genuinely differentiated here, since no other lab runs more than two at scale. That breadth is a real negotiating lever on every one of them. Evidence the porting cost is now low enough to make it credible: **Anthropic Head of Compute Tom Brown** described using **Claude over a single weekend with "/goal" to bring up Anthropic's internal Claude inference stack on AMD hardware** — the concrete datapoint behind SemiAnalysis upgrading AMD from "0% chance" (2024) to "non-zero" (AMD 2.0) to **"a great chance of success"**, and the sharper version of the Tom Brown "past the CUDA moat days" line logged above. Provenance note: SemiAnalysis had **predicted the AMD-Anthropic pairing in an April-2026 institutional note** from its Accelerator & HBM team (and on X on 07-20) — i.e. a scored forecast, not a reaction. See [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]]. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **🆕 READ-THROUGH — the 2GW commitment is now on an AMD EARNINGS CALL, with the 1H27 first-gigawatt timing intact, and Su volunteered the delivery path: THROUGH CSPs, NOT SELF-BUILD (AMD Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT, 2026-08-04).** The 2GW / 1H27 shape is already on this page from the 07-22 announcement and the 07-23 roundtable; what is net-new is **(a) AMD reaffirming it in prepared remarks on a reported quarter — the first time it carries earnings-call standing rather than event standing — and (b) how Su described the ramp mechanics.** Verbatim, prepared remarks: _"we announced a new strategic partnership with Anthropic. **Anthropic will deploy UP TO TWO GIGAWATTS of MI450-series GPUs in Helios, with deployment of the FIRST GIGAWATT BEGINNING IN THE FIRST HALF OF 2027.** The partnership includes a multi-year joint engineering collaboration using Claude to optimize workloads for Instinct GPUs and accelerate ROCm software development."_ ★ **The delivery-path detail is the genuinely new thing, and it changes how the commitment should show up in a model.** In Q&A, Su named **"our strategic anchor customers in OpenAI, Meta and Anthropic — they are ALL IN THE PROCESS OF RAMPING,"** and described that ramp as **"very much aligning their data center build outs and their work with their CLOUD SERVICE PROVIDERS, together with our ramp"** — i.e. **Anthropic's AMD capacity is expected to land THROUGH CSPs rather than through Anthropic-owned build.** Asked separately (Jim Schneider, GS) about MI450 customer diversity, Su was explicit: the frontier labs **"will be consuming through a number of CSPs… both hyperscalers and others."** ⚠️ **Read: the 2GW is a compute commitment, not necessarily an Anthropic-leased-datacenter commitment — consistent with the multi-cloud/fungible posture this page already carries, and it means the capacity may appear inside a CSP's capex and RPO rather than Anthropic's own. It also makes CSP data-center readiness, not AMD silicon, the gating variable on the 1H27 start.** Two supporting items from the same call: **rocm.ai "lets developers use today's leading coding agents, including Claude, Codex and Cursor, to create port and optimize code for Instinct"** (the productised form of the reciprocal engineering leg already on this page), and AMD is **"working closely with the leading AI labs, including OpenAI, Anthropic, Meta and others to optimize ROCm for their models."** Finally, a mild dilution of the "frontier labs are the whole story" framing: Su flagged **"lots of customers who are interested in Helios at a more regular scale than gigawatt scale"** — i.e. AMD expects real non-frontier Helios diversity, which slightly reduces Anthropic's negotiating uniqueness on that platform. ⚠️ Machine transcript — product names normalised (MI450-series, ROCm, rocm.ai, EPYC); no garbled token quoted. See [[AMD]], [[OPENAI]], [[META]]. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMD_2Q.html)
- **Micron memory deal implies an in-house ASIC (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** the recently-announced Anthropic–Micron partnership is a **combination of server DRAM + enterprise SSD in 2027 plus HBM in 2028** — and the HBM component specifically **implies Anthropic is developing its own in-house ASIC** (you only need HBM if you're buying/building accelerators, not just renting cloud capacity). Avril adds that **Anthropic is pursuing the same deal structure with SK Hynix and Samsung** — i.e. it is locking up direct memory allocation across all three makers, consistent with a multi-silicon roadmap (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **ASIC / Broadcom (UBS, Tim Arcuri/AVGO callback, 2026-06-04):** Broadcom's Anthropic XPU commitment is **5 GW, all Broadcom (no MediaTek)**; the **Anthropic + OpenAI SPV** points to ~20 GW, leaving ~15 GW for the two parked into 2028 — calendar-year (not fiscal) figures. Broadcom frames **OpenAI's compute demand as similar to Anthropic's, but with lower revenue visibility**; revenue-per-gigawatt messaging has drifted from ">20" to "20" to "10–20, varies by customer." Anthropic remains the higher-visibility of the two ASIC anchors (UBS "AVGO callback", 2026-06-04). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_04_ubs_avgo_4_jun_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_04_ubs_avgo_4_jun_26.html)
- **TPU resale economics (hyperscaler-recap expert, 2026-04-30):** Google records the **Broadcom→Google→Anthropic TPU resale gross** (Google takes a ~10–15% / ~$2–3k-per-chip license markup, books the full grossed-up amount in revenue and Google Cloud backlog, no capex). Anthropic's TPU orders: ~1 GW in 2026 (the initial $10bn + a late-26 $11bn Broadcom order = ~$21bn ≈ 1 GW) plus **~3 GW incremental for 2027** — ~4 GW total of TPUs; the grossed-up TPU dollars (~$11.5bn in 4Q25, ~$85bn in 1Q26) inflate Google Cloud's reported backlog. Google also agreed to **provide credit backstops for third-party data centers / power** tied to these Anthropic TPU agreements (per Google's 10-Q) (hyperscaler recap, 2026-04-30). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html)
- **Neocloud compute (Cantor CoreWeave call, 2026-06-18):** Anthropic signed only a **small ~$2–3bn / 70 MW CoreWeave deal (Apr ~10–11)** — read as far below its true need ("Anthropic is not in the business of trying to find 70 megawatts"); Cantor expects a **sizable Anthropic CoreWeave deal (~$20–30bn)** to be announced alongside a big 2Q RPO beat. The **Google↔SpaceX ~$10/GPU-hr H100 deal** (well above market) is read as effectively channeling **Colossus capacity to Anthropic** for near-term IPO-revenue compute (Cantor CoreWeave call, 2026-06-18). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_call_coreweave_cantor.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_call_coreweave_cantor.html)
- **GPU / chronic compute-shortage (BofA Vivek Arya NVDA preview, 2026-05-19):** **OpenAI + Anthropic together comprise ~40–50% of the leading hyperscalers' backlog**, and (pre-IPO) are "very motivated to show growth" — the demand pull underwriting ~12–18 months of capacity. Anthropic is "always scrambling for more capacity" — its service "slows down in the middle of the day"; Arya expects NVDA to want to **invest in Anthropic directly** (a prior "regret" it missed) and/or help via neoclouds / XAI Colossus inference capacity. NVDA's Ian Buck (LinkedIn) cited Vera CPUs delivered to **Oracle, SpaceX, OpenAI and Anthropic** (BofA NVDA preview, 2026-05-19). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_19_nvda_vivek_19_may_26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_19_nvda_vivek_19_may_26.html)
- **Compute commitments:** AWS primary cloud + Trainium/Bedrock anchor driving AWS margin expansion vs Azure/GCP (SemiAnalysis, 2026-05-27, `research_library`); ~$25B Mississippi cluster; multi-cloud across Trainium/TPU/GPU. AWS treats Anthropic's training-compute spend inside its own AWS revenue line (UBS OpenAI note, 2025-09-02). Barclays' AI-capex framework models **Anthropic compute expense at ~$8B (2025) → $22B (2026E) → $46B (2027E) → ~$95B (2030E)** — ~one-third of the combined OpenAI+Anthropic compute pool (OpenAI's is ~3x larger), with Anthropic's smaller forecast attributed to its narrower text/enterprise/coding focus and multi-architecture (GPU/Trainium/TPU/custom) efficiency; training-compute spend for the two leaders peaks ~2029 (~15.5 GW), driving an **industry AI-capex peak ~2028** (Barclays AI framework, 2026-03-11). JPM's earlier cruder cut had Anthropic paying cloud providers ~$1.3-3.1B inference in 2025, rising to ~$2.1-4.1B in 2026 (JPM initiation, 2025-09-18). xAI/SpaceX struck a compute deal with Anthropic monetizing at ~$22–23B/GW/yr (BG2Pod, 2026-06-12). JPMorgan's Harlan Sur has flagged an open question on whether **Broadcom is involved in Anthropic ASIC design** (`_briefings` 2026-06-18). Bernstein's 1Q26 hyperscaler review notes Anthropic's **>$100B deal with AWS is excluded from AWS's reported $364B backlog** (i.e., AWS backlog understates committed Anthropic compute), and flags Anthropic appearing for the first time (alongside OpenAI) in CY25 Gartner PaaS data — evidence the private labs are now scaled enough to register as standalone cloud/PaaS players (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **BACKFILL / HISTORICAL — where Anthropic stood in Sept-2025, and the structural claim that has held up best (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", Ontiveros/Patel/Kourabi/Xie, 2025-09-03 — ingested 2026-07-24, ~11 months old; supersedes nothing current on this page):** the Sep-2025 baseline, useful mostly as the origin point for the curves the rest of this page tracks. **Revenue $1B → $5B annualised during 2025 (5x YTD)**; the **~$13B round at a $183B valuation** (vs $965B post-money today). Crucially for the cloud-mix debate: **as of Q2-2025 Anthropic's cloud spend was still "over 2x smaller than that of OpenAI,"** and a large share of it was going to **Google Cloud** — Anthropic's early investor ($300M round in late-2022) and preferred cloud through 2023–24 before the expanded AWS deal — with SemiAnalysis believing **most of Anthropic's *inference* was served by Google Cloud on TPU** ("having the world's best inference system (TPU) is a key competitive advantage"). That is the direct ancestor of the 07-13 Archera read on this page (majority of Claude production PTU tokens still sold through Google Vertex) — the Google-inference tilt is an 18-month-old structural fact, not a 2026 development. AWS-relationship chronology as SemiAnalysis lays it out: **$1.25B (expandable to $4B) in Sept-2023**, expanded **March-2024** with the Trainium/Inferentia commitment, then **a further $4B in Nov-2024** naming AWS the primary LLM training partner. **The structural claim worth keeping — and the one that has aged best: "Trainium2 is converging toward an Anthropic custom-silicon program."** Anthropic was already "heavily involved in all Trainium design decisions" and "essentially use[s] Amazon's Annapurna Labs as a custom silicon partner," making it — **alongside Google DeepMind, the only two AI labs benefiting from tight hardware-software co-design.** Read forward: that framing anticipated by ~10 months both the 07-02 Information report of Anthropic's own early-stage chip and the 07-14 Samsung-Foundry engagement already logged above; the co-design thesis is the through-line. Mechanism given at the time: Trainium2 loses to GB200 on nearly every spec (3.85x FP16 FLOPs, 2.75x memory bandwidth, 3.1x aggregate scale-up bandwidth) but wins on **TCO per million tokens and TCO per TB/s of memory bandwidth** — and Anthropic's roadmap is "more memory-bandwidth-bound than FLOPs bound" because it is "the most aggressive AI Lab on scaling post-training techniques like Reinforcement Learning." Anthropic's ramp was expected to make it not only the sole large external Trainium2 user but **materially larger than Amazon's own internal needs (Bedrock, Alexa, etc.)**. **Product/moat as called then:** Claude Opus 4.1 led SWE-Bench and Claude remained "the top model in Cursor" against GPT-5 ("we see Cursor as the ultimate indicator of which model nails the balance between capability and usability"); the coding position was already framed as **creating a data moat and a flywheel effect** that OpenAI's tighter-privacy Copilot base could not replicate — the origin of the coding-flywheel argument the 07-09 note later grounded in RL-environment data buying. Risk named even then, and unchanged: **"lower network effects and entry barriers compared to ChatGPT's consumer moat"** — which SemiAnalysis argued "likely makes Anthropic more paranoid than any other lab." Ecosystem datapoint for the adoption curve Anthropic was riding: **Cursor took 750 days to $100M ARR, then broke $250M in 45 days and $500M in 15 days.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_09_03_ENC_Amazons_AI_Resurgence_AWS_Anthropics_Multi-Gigawatt_Trainium_Expansion_email.html)
- **Amazon partnership renegotiated — token-based billing (Rothschild & Co, TMT Daily sales, 2026-06-30 / 2026-07-01 detail).** Anthropic has renegotiated its Amazon partnership: **starting next year Amazon will be billed based on tokens processed rather than compute hours**, materially raising Amazon's AI costs — the concrete mechanic behind the 06-30 "flexing pricing power over Amazon" framing already on this page. This is prompting some Amazon engineers to **"distill" Anthropic models ahead of the change** (a defensive move to reduce dependence before the cost step-up lands). Amazon relies on Anthropic for **Alexa shopping, the Kiro coding tool, and the Quick workspace assistant**, and will evaluate cheaper alternatives including its in-house **Nova models** as it reassesses the relationship under the new billing structure (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily, sales, 2026-06-30).
- **Distillation-guardrails spreading to a second megacap customer (Rothschild & Co, 2026-06-30).** Separate from the Amazon dynamic, **Meta is restricting its engineers' use of Anthropic's Claude Code** (alongside OpenAI's Codex) within its applied-AI engineering division over "distillation" concerns; the policy has been in place since May 2026 — i.e. two of Anthropic's largest enterprise/hyperscaler counterparties are now independently guarding against model distillation by their own staff (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily, sales, 2026-06-30).
- **Anthropic goes on the offensive on "distillation attacks" against the Chinese labs — an IP/moat defense (BofA · Brian Fenske, 2026-07-17).** As Kimi K3 landed, BofA resurfaced Anthropic's escalating public stance: Anthropic has **publicly accused Alibaba, Moonshot, DeepSeek and Minimax of "distillation attacks"** — illicitly extracting Claude's capabilities to train their own models. Its **Feb 23 2026 blog ("Detecting and preventing distillation attacks")** claimed industrial-scale campaigns (**Minimax ~13M exchanges the most; Moonshot/Kimi ~3.4M via hundreds of fraudulent accounts** attributed by request-metadata to senior Moonshot staff, later attempting to reconstruct Claude's reasoning traces); on **Jun 24 2026** Anthropic sent a letter to US officials calling an Alibaba effort "**the largest known distillation attack on Anthropic to date**." FinTwit corroboration (@PatrickMoorhead / @zephyr_z9): **Kimi K3 appears to have distilled Fable** (unbanned Jul 1), "so big training infra is still required for the leading edge." Read: reframes the "cheap Chinese open models" narrative as partly derivative of US-frontier output, and is Anthropic's active defense of its capability moat — but also underscores the leakage risk (why customers like Meta guard Claude Code internally, above). (BofA · Brian Fenske, 2026-07-17; see [themes/china-export.md](themes/china-export.md), [themes/tokenmaxxing.md](themes/tokenmaxxing.md))
- **Compute-ceiling / xAI Colossus resale confirmation (JPMorgan, Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-06-30; Vital Knowledge, Adam Crisafulli, 2026-07-01).** JPMorgan reports **xAI sold its Colossus 1 compute capacity to Anthropic for $1.25bn/month**, containing 220,000+ NVIDIA GPUs (H100, H200, GB200) — consistent with the Nomura/Barron's SpaceX↔Anthropic figures already on this page — and flags Anthropic's **ARR ceiling as reportedly compute-constrained** (JPMorgan, Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-06-30). Vital Knowledge separately notes SpaceX's xAI reportedly selling excess compute to external customers including **Google and Anthropic**, drawing a parallel to Meta's newly-announced "Meta Compute" external-cloud pivot the same day (Vital Knowledge / Adam Crisafulli, 2026-07-01).
- **Own AI chip — early-stage program + Samsung fab talks (The Information, 2026-07-02).** Anthropic has begun **early-stage work on its own AI chip** and held talks with **Samsung Electronics as a potential manufacturing partner** — the first *explicit* confirmation of an in-house accelerator effort. This converts what was previously an inference (StoneX/TrendForce, 06-23: the Micron/SK Hynix/Samsung HBM-in-2028 memory deals *imply* an own ASIC, since you only need HBM if you build/buy accelerators) into direct reporting. It sits alongside — not in place of — the multi-silicon rent strategy (Trainium/TPU/GPU) and the Broadcom XPU design relationship (UBS/Arcuri: 5 GW all-Broadcom); Samsung enters as a candidate foundry, relevant to the [SAMSUNG](SAMSUNG.md) foundry-utilization story. Answers, at least partially, Harlan Sur's standing open question on whether Anthropic is designing its own silicon (The Information, 2026-07-02).
- **Distribution — Teams agent + OpenRouter usage/spend leadership.** Anthropic is reportedly developing a **Claude agent feature for Microsoft Teams**, following the Slack/Salesforce partnership announced the prior week letting Slack customers summon Claude for workplace tasks (The Information, Applied AI / Laura Bratton, 2026-06-30 — corroborating the Teams item already logged in the 06-30 intra-quarter row; re-confirmed 2026-07-06 via MS TMT / Bernstein TMT). Separately, **Claude Opus 4.7 was the only model ranking top-5 in both volume usage and spending on OpenRouter in June 2026** (JPMorgan, Data Center Watch / Joseph Cardoso et al., 2026-07-01).
- **WULF / WYKF compute lease — 400MW Kentucky data center (MS TMT · Thomas Wigg / Bernstein TMT · Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-06).** Anthropic signed a **20-year lease (~$19B)** to use a WYKF-operated data center in **Kentucky, ~400MW capacity, first power delivery 2H'27** — implying **~22% yield** at the ~$8-10/watt capex figure plus **~85% NOI margin**; WULF shares rose **+16-20%** on the news (BBG / MS TMT; Bernstein TMT cited ~$20B / 20yr). A major frontier-training capacity add outside the AWS/Google/Azure/SpaceX stack already logged on this page (MS TMT · Wigg / Bernstein TMT · Seidman, 2026-07-06).
- **AMZN partnership — alternatives under evaluation (The Information, via MS TMT / Bernstein TMT, 2026-07-06).** Building on the 06-30 Rothschild readout that Amazon will be billed on **tokens processed rather than compute hours starting next year** (materially raising Amazon's costs), The Information adds that **AMZN is now evaluating alternatives — OpenAI models and its own Nova — to reduce Claude dependence** (The Information via MS TMT, 2026-07-06). Cuts both ways: confirms Anthropic's pricing leverage but flags counterparty substitution risk at its largest equity backer/primary cloud.
- **China enforcement tightening (FT, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06).** Anthropic is tightening enforcement against Chinese access to Claude — companies had used overseas entities, cloud providers, VPNs and "transfer station" services to bypass restrictions (FT via MS TMT, 2026-07-06). Consistent with the export-control/China thread already on the page.
- **No US-government stake discussed (FT, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06).** Anthropic has reportedly **not** discussed a US-government equity stake — in contrast to OpenAI, which reportedly discussed a **5% stake** (FT via MS TMT, 2026-07-06). A governance/policy contrast relevant to the Gurley/@Hesamation "lobbying-for-protection" debate.
- **Enterprise displacement — CRM read-through (The Information, 2026-07-06).** A report that small firms are using Claude to **replace Salesforce** — a competicao read-through for CRM (The Information, 2026-07-06). Directional evidence that agentic Claude workflows are starting to substitute for packaged SaaS at the small-business end.
- **Developer-platform architecture & roadmap — the "three-layer cake" (Anthropic @ Sequoia fireside — platform leads Caitlin & Angela, 2026-07-14):** the clearest first-party articulation of how Anthropic productizes Claude for builders (internal + external, deliberately kept the same primitives). Three ascending abstraction layers: **(1) knowledge** — the messages API + standards (MCP, skills, memory) that expose how the model thinks/does tool-calls; **(2) execution** — a low-level harness + managed infrastructure, today's high-level product being **Claude Managed Agents** (spawns governed sandboxes, stores/resumes transcript sessions, handles prompt-caching/context-window management out of the box); **(3) coordination** — the emerging layer of **"strategies"/meta-harnesses** built on the thesis that "tokens aren't fungible — a token has a job" (advising vs executing vs reflecting-to-memory vs best-of-n verification), which the roadmap is now moving toward. Design choices: not "precious" about running on Anthropic infra — **self-hosted sandboxes partnered with Modal, Vercel, Cloudflare and Amazon's new micro-VMs**, plus **MCP tunnels** to reach MCP servers behind a firewall. Verticalization is explicit (**finance, legal**) and biased to **token-hungry** use-cases (coding the archetype — "you finish a turn and want to do more"). Products framed as packagings of the same primitives: **Claude tag** (an "org-level harness" accessed via Slack — "the interface can swap, the context engineering underneath is the moat"), **Claude for financial services** (skills packages), **Claude design** (code-as-the-medium for generative design). On models: explicitly **not building a cross-vendor router — designing the platform for Claude only** (harnesses should be tuned to the model family, echoing Vercel's harness-agent approach). [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_anth_on_sequoia_14_jul_26.html)
- **"Token maxxing → token rationalization" — Anthropic's own framing (Anthropic @ Sequoia fireside, 2026-07-14):** asked about the shift from token-maxxing to rationalization, the platform team endorsed it as a "normal, natural cycle" — as models get more capable, the next optimization dimension after intelligence is **cost or speed**. Their guidance to enterprises: **don't stop AI usage** (the wrong move; often the spend erupted via "shadow IT" as employees installed Claude Code themselves) — instead design a **complexity-router strategy** (assess task complexity → route hard tasks to a big model, easy tasks to cheaper models), which Anthropic intends to offer as one of its composable "strategies" **within the Claude model family**. Corroborates the model-layer down-tiering pressure in the token-optimization bear reads on the page, but reframed as a within-Claude routing opportunity Anthropic wants to own rather than a share loss. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_anth_on_sequoia_14_jul_26.html)
- **Channel share + capacity read — Claude ~70/30 over GPT, but served mostly off Google TPU (Archera expert call — ex-AWS operator, 2026-07-13):** an ex-AWS operator's book puts **client interest at ~70/30 Claude:GPT** (down from more Claude-dominant after the GPT-5.5 resurgence), with Claude having won the codegen use-case and the enterprise long-tail first (buyers procure it inside their VPC via Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry for data-privacy, not direct). Key capacity nuance: as of start-2026 **the majority of Claude production PTU tokens are sold through Google Vertex** — Google's deep TPU well can serve even leading-edge Opus/Fable, whereas **Sonnet runs fine on AWS Trainium2 but Opus 4.28/Fable show capacity issues on Amazon** (so Amazon still serves those off Nvidia GPUs, making its Claude token supply less fungible than Google's). Two bear flags: (1) **open-source competition — GLM 5.2** has hit the RL loop that made Opus special and, at ~¼ the cost, could over time squeeze the labs' codegen token share "down into 20%"; (2) enterprises are **wary of Anthropic's "propensity to compete with its partners,"** pushing them to keep an owned open-source model in a router alongside Claude. [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_archera_13_jul_26.html)
- **IPO financials sneak-peek — >$60B ARR, 3Q26 profit >$1B, $6T base case (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic 3Q26 Profit Over $1B", 2026-07-08).** SemiAnalysis's Tokenomics team (bottoms-up by SKU/tier/customer, "confirmed" by a recent WSJ piece) frames the confidential Jun-1 IPO filing: Anthropic ended 2025 at **$9B ARR → $30B by 1Q26** (Jan +$3B, Feb +$7B, Mar +$11B) → **>$60B today (3Q26)** — the first AI lab of this scale to IPO. **3Q26 GAAP EBIT ~$1B (6% margin)** vs OpenAI at ~−100% EBIT margin (Anthropic likely "does not want to be profitable" but compute constraints cap reinvestment). **Revenue mix: usage-based API 75–85% of revenue; subscriptions just 15% of ARR; only ~5% from Consumer subs (vs OpenAI ~40%).** Cumulative training cost of ~$8B since 1Q24 yielded a $60B ARR business (an "extremely impressive ROIC"). **NRR ~500%** (Krishna Rao on Invest Like the Best): of the $30B 1Q26 ARR, ~$12B came from customers present ≥1yr prior who were ~$2B in 1Q25 → **~$18B of new-customer ARR still ramping**; **NNARR now $10B+/month** (Fable the next accelerant, targeting >$10B/mo in 2H26). **Free MAUs ~55–60M, 9% of consumer MAUs paying** (vs OpenAI ~950M WAU, ~6% paying); Claude Code **>7% of total GitHub commits** — the driver of the 1Q26 reacceleration. Plans $20/$100/$200/mo; **avg paying US consumer ~$45/mo** (card-panel). Unit-econ detail: **blended inference GM mid-60s% (vs −94% in 2024); Opus 4.8 API-token GM 80%+; ARR-per-MW reaching ~$60M later this year vs ~$16M 9 months ago; 2Q26 EBTIT margin ~36%.** ≥60% of compute is earmarked for training/internal today; long-run ~25% of revenue on training compute (48%/52% training/inference split by 2030). Steady-state 30–40% EBIT/FCF margins possible (GM into mid-70s). >$100B raised. **Base-case valuation 20x 2027 ending-ARR of $300B (~$15B/mo NNARR in 2027) → ~$6T EV, the world's largest company** — "Anthropic as the first $6T company as a base-case." Compute is the governor: SemiAnalysis sees OpenAI+Anthropic >100GW of unconstrained demand by 2030 (>90GW of net adds through 2030 vs 2.5GW in 2025 / 5GW in 2026; ~6GW available today) — the IPO's purpose is capital access to bid up compute. Risk flagged: more Fable-style government delays (regulatory lock-in doesn't help when hyperscaler labs are catching up + Chinese labs distilling) (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic 3Q26 Profit Over $1B: The Anthropic IPO Financials Sneak Peak", Brookhart/Huang/Patel, 2026-07-08). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Fw-_Anthropic_3Q26_Profit_Over_1B-_The_Anthropic_IPO_Financials_Sneak_Peak.html) · [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md)
- **SemiAnalysis 07-08 — exhibits & P&L detail (chart transcription).** The bullet above was ingested from the email text; the note itself carries **18 charts/tables**, incl. SemiAnalysis's full **consolidated quarterly P&L** for Anthropic (1Q24→4Q26), transcribed here: [figures sidecar](_data/figures/Semianalysis_Anthropic_IPO_Financials_Sneak_Peek.md) · archived [source PDF](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Anthropic_IPO_Financials_Sneak_Peek.pdf). Quant not in the text readout: **quarterly GAAP recognized revenue** (their model, "WSJ-validated") **$4.9B (1Q26) → $11.5B (2Q26) → $20.1B (3Q26E) → $30.2B (4Q26E)** (~$120B annualized exit-26 — distinct from the >$60B *exit run-rate* ARR headline). P&L walk 3Q26E: gross profit $12.9B (mid-60s GM) − training $7.6B − other opex $4.0B = **GAAP OI +$1.2B (6%)** — GAAP OI turns positive in **3Q26**, but *adjusted* (ex-SBC) OI was already positive from ~**2Q26** (p.3 "We Were Right About Anthropic" exhibit). The **EBTIT** metric (= GAAP OI + training, i.e. pre-training-reinvestment) turns positive in **1Q26**, ~**36% in 2Q26**, ~**$27.6B FY26E**; cumulative EBTIT edge over OpenAI ~**$250B through 2028**, leaving **$160B of capital-after-COGS to reinvest in 2027 vs $92B at OpenAI** — the quantified core of the "out-invest OpenAI" thesis. **TaaS/indirect now 15-20% of ARR** (vs 5-10% a quarter ago); total TaaS market ~**$28B ARR (2Q26)**, ~85% across the big-3 (Bedrock ~$29B / Vertex ~$24B / Foundry ~$5B annualized by 4Q26E). **Coding vertical: Anthropic 1P coding ARR ~$92B annualized by 4Q26E vs OpenAI ~$41B**; **Meta likely the single largest customer at just 3-5% of revenue** (9-figure spend) — no single-account concentration. Pricing exhibit (Source: Anthropic): **Sonnet 5 steps up $2/$10 → $3/$15 per MTok on 2026-09-01** (cited as evidence even lower-tier models can raise price). **Provenance caveat:** same SemiAnalysis Tokenomics team behind the 05-27/06-30 notes already on this page — a mark-up of the model that already underpins these figures, not an independent check — and the 3Q26 GAAP-profit figure is a *nowcast* (the quarter had just begun at publication) (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-08).
- **SpaceX/Colossus 1 rental economics — the $/W math (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07).** JPM sizes the May-2026 SpaceX↔Anthropic deal: Anthropic rents **100% of Colossus 1** (first tranche 220k NVIDIA GPUs, scaling in ~110k / ~220MW increments), at **$1.25bn/month**, terminable by either party on 90 days' notice after an initial three-month period. JPM's blended rate estimate is **~$29/W** (a Hopper/Blackwell mix — ~$13/W for the Hoppers, ~$50/W for Blackwell GB200/300, consistent with the Google comparator), with Anthropic paying ~$330m/month for the Blackwell portion. The compute "is presumably to increase Claude's" serving capacity. JPM also flags the **Anthropic–Amazon "Project Rainier" New Carlisle, IN facility at ~910MW** as the #2-largest AI facility operating globally in 3Q26 (behind SpaceX's Colossus 2 ~950MW) — the physical anchor of the AWS/Trainium relationship already on this page (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_SpaceX_The_Next_Fron_2026-07-07_5359413.html)
- **Capacity-deal $/GW read-through — Anthropic/SpaceX at ">$40bn per GW" (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07).** In its hyperscaler capex/capacity/monetization deep-dive, BofA (Justin Post) cites **recent capacity deals signed by Anthropic and Google with SpaceX at over $40bn per GW (up to ~$50bn/GW for specialized AI capacity)** — used as evidence that hyperscaler Cloud revenue-per-GW estimates ($10-17bn/GW for AWS/Google Cloud) understate the value of AI capacity. Read-through for Anthropic: its named compute deals are being marked by the sell-side at the top end of the capacity-value range — a demand/pricing-power signal for the lab, but note the figure is a *per-GW deal-value* framing that runs **above** the other marks already on this page (Dylan Patel/SemiAnalysis: SpaceX/Colossus GPU deal ~$25bn/GW; BG2Pod: xAI/SpaceX monetizes ~$22-23bn/GW/yr; Trainium <$10bn/GW). The spread reflects silicon type (specialized AI/GPU capacity commands the premium) and framing (total deal value per GW vs annual monetization/yr) — retained as a distinct BofA source estimate, not a supersession (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_Capex.html)
- **Broadcom-side confirmation of the XPV rack lease + ASIC-program non-answer (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07):** on the JPM-hosted Hock Tan call, Broadcom confirmed the mechanics of the **$35B XPV/SPV** already on this page from Anthropic's side — the SPV (Apollo/Blackstone equity + private/insurance debt) **buys the racks and leases them to Anthropic on a 5-yr term**, with Broadcom selling only the chips into it (its P&L stays chip-margins-only). Hock characterized Anthropic as currently **renting Google TPUs** (consistent with the multi-silicon rent strategy), and — asked directly about a **potential future Anthropic in-house ASIC program — explicitly declined to comment** ("this question, I really am not qualified to answer, and nor is it in my interest to answer, so I shall decline"). I.e. Broadcom would neither confirm nor deny an Anthropic XPU design engagement, leaving Harlan Sur's standing open question unresolved from the supplier side even as The Information (07-02) reported Anthropic's own early-stage chip effort. (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07)
- **Micron–Anthropic deal = supply guarantee for an equity stake (vendor financing) (Bernstein LTA analysis, 2026-07-09):** Bernstein's memory long-term-agreement (LTA) work characterizes the Micron–Anthropic arrangement as **Micron providing a supply guarantee in exchange for an Anthropic equity stake** — i.e. vendor financing, not a cash prepay/deposit. Bernstein's read is that this structure **"didn't reduce the risk for [Micron] investors,"** since the "payment" is illiquid private equity rather than cash upfront. A sharper characterization of the Micron/SK Hynix/Samsung memory-lockup deals already on this page (StoneX/TrendForce 06-23, which flagged the HBM-in-2028 component as implying an in-house Anthropic ASIC). (Bernstein LTA analysis, 2026-07-09)
- **Cash-flow-positive confirmation (Dylan Patel podcast, via @jukan05, 2026-07-10):** Anthropic **turned free-cash-flow positive in Q2 2026 and has entered profitability** — both **April and May were profitable and cash-flow positive**, with June (not yet closed at recording) trending the same way; **ARR has now surpassed $50B, gross margins >70%.** Firms up the page's prior profitability marks (adjusted-OI positive from ~2Q26; GAAP EBIT +$1B in 3Q26) with an explicit *cash-flow*-positive print. The >$50B ARR is a floor consistent with — not a supersession of — the **>$60B (3Q26)** / Yipit **$69B** marks already on the page. (Dylan Patel via @jukan05, 2026-07-10) [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- **NVIDIA's share of Anthropic compute rising toward ~50% (Morgan Stanley NVDA NDR w/ Jensen Huang, via @Midnight_Captl, 2026-07-10):** on NVIDIA's NDR, AI labs were framed as **~20% of total NVDA demand** currently; on Anthropic specifically, "one of the more prominent frontier models was developed mostly on an **ASIC**, so NVIDIA had minimal exposure, but that has **risen to close to 50%**," while other frontier models remain mostly on NVDA — i.e. NVIDIA's share of Anthropic's compute has climbed **from negligible to ~50%** in a short window, a read-through on Anthropic's Trainium/ASIC-vs-NVDA silicon mix (see [NVDA.md](NVDA.md)). (MS NVDA NDR w/ Jensen via @Midnight_Captl, 2026-07-10)
- **Multi-decade compute contract — Bernstein cross-read (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-11):** in its SPCX sales note, Bernstein cites **Anthropic as an example of a multi-decade compute contract** anchoring SpaceX's neocloud / orbital-AI ambitions — a contract-durability signal consistent with the SpaceX↔Anthropic Colossus arrangement ($1.25bn/mo) already on this page. See [SPCX.md](SPCX.md). (Bernstein · Seidman, 2026-07-11)
- **Google-stake mark-up quantified — ~$80bn one-time 2Q'26 gain on a ~14% Anthropic stake (BofA · Justin Post, "Alphabet", 2026-07-06):** BofA estimates Alphabet books an **~$80bn one-time mark-to-market benefit in 2Q'26** from revaluing its Anthropic stake (Anthropic's valuation stepping from **$380bn in 1Q → $965bn in 2Q**), with press reports putting Alphabet's ownership at **~14%**. BofA's supporting demand read: **Anthropic ARR estimated ~$45-47bn exiting 2Q'26 vs ~$19bn exiting March** — consistent with (and an independent corroboration of) the >$47B May-26 print already on this page — feeding Google Cloud backlog "that suggests at least $230bn in contracted revenues over the next 8 quarters." Frames Anthropic's private-round appreciation as a **direct, sizeable P&L event for a public holder** (GOOGL) — the cleanest read yet of the equity-stake economics. [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BofA_Justin_Post_on_Alphabet.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BofA_Justin_Post_on_Alphabet.html)
- **Forward ARR call via SDK-download signal — ~$70bn by Sept, >$110bn by year-end (Arete Research "AI Hardware: Rising Tide", 2026-04-14):** Arete finds a **>90% correlation between SDK-download data and reported ARR for both OpenAI and Anthropic, with a ~6-month lead** — and on that basis projects **Anthropic and OpenAI each on track to hit ~$70bn ARR by September 2026 and >$110bn each by year-end.** An independent, methodologically-distinct forward mark that brackets the SemiAnalysis >$60B (3Q26) / Yipit $69B figures on the high side into year-end. (Arete Research, 2026-04-14) [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Arete_Research_AI_Hardware_Rising_Tide.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Arete_Research_AI_Hardware_Rising_Tide.html)
- **Frontier-lab unit economics — the per-GW ROIC framing (Cantor Fitzgerald "Framing The ROIC Debates In AI", 2026-04-08):** Cantor's bottoms-up on the AI labs (OpenAI + Anthropic blended): at the headline level AI labs generate **~$10B revenue per GW vs ~$11B compute expense per GW → contribution margin currently negative**; but the picture inverts on the *monetized* slice — inference-deployed compute earns **~$25B/GW at ~60% gross margin (FY26E)**, and **excluding the compute used for free-tier usage, monetization runs ~$35B/GW.** Free-tier consumes **~30% of inference compute — ~40% at OpenAI (heavy ChatGPT free base) vs only ~15% at Anthropic** — structurally advantaging Anthropic's enterprise mix. **>60% of total compute is allocated to model training**; frontier-model *capability* lead lasts **<60 days** (leap-frogged ~every 2 months) even as new model *families* ship ~annually — so a failed 3-6-month training run carries heavy capital + opportunity cost. Cantor sees inference GM rising further on free→paid mix shift + monetization/architecture gains. (Cantor Fitzgerald, 2026-04-08) [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Hyperscalers_ROIC_Debates_8_apr_2026.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Hyperscalers_ROIC_Debates_8_apr_2026.html)
- **Enterprise deal-structure & early-2026 momentum (historical backdrop — Mizuho "On the Frontier: OpenAI & Anthropic", Marc Bachner, 2026-03-15):** as of early 2026 Mizuho counted **Anthropic with ≥9 customers spending >$100M annually and ~500 customers spending $1M+/yr (vs only 12 such customers two years prior)** — the trajectory behind the "1,000+ paying $1M+/yr" figure now on this page — with **Claude Code already at $2.5B+ ARR** and ARR having roughly **doubled to ~$20B in the first ~2 months of 2026.** Frontier labs "commanding an ever-greater share of IT budgets," which Mizuho flagged could pressure enterprise spend on other tech vendors. (Historical — superseded by current ARR/customer figures above; retained for the >$100M-customer-count structure.) (Mizuho, 2026-03-15) [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/On_the_Frontier__OpenAI_Anthropic_and_the_Consumer_and_En.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/On_the_Frontier__OpenAI_Anthropic_and_the_Consumer_and_En.html)
- **Enterprise "token budgeting" — the specific bills behind the ROI reassessment (FundaAI "LLM: 26H1 Update (Part 1)", 2026-06-23):** the concrete anecdotes underpinning the tokenmaxxing→budgeting shift already on this page: **Salesforce's annual Anthropic bill ran ~$300m** (Benioff now pushing a smart router + "Agentic Work Units" that measure work completed, not tokens); **one large company hit a ~$500m/month Claude bill** having set no cap; **Uber burned its entire 2026 AI-coding budget in 4 months → capped tools like Claude Code/Cursor at $1,500/person/month**; and **Microsoft cancelled some internal Claude Code licenses, migrating its Experiences+Devices engineers back to GitHub Copilot CLI**.
