  **The 2GW / first-gigawatt-1H27 shape was already on the page three ways (07-22 announcement, AMD roundtable 07-23, SemiAnalysis 07-24) — so what was folded in is deliberately only the delta.** ① **AMD reaffirmed it in PREPARED REMARKS ON A REPORTED QUARTER with the 1H27 timing intact** — earnings-call standing rather than event standing, verbatim: "Anthropic will deploy up to two gigawatts of MI450-series GPUs in Helios, with deployment of the first gigawatt beginning in the first half of 2027." ② ★ **NEW AND MODEL-RELEVANT — the delivery path. In Q&A Su named "our strategic anchor customers in OpenAI, Meta and Anthropic — they are ALL IN THE PROCESS OF RAMPING" and described the ramp as "very much aligning their data center build outs and their work with their CLOUD SERVICE PROVIDERS," and separately said the frontier labs "will be consuming through a number of CSPs… both hyperscalers and others." Read: Anthropic's AMD capacity is expected to land THROUGH CSPs rather than self-build — so it may sit inside a CSP's capex/RPO rather than Anthropic's, and CSP data-center readiness (not AMD silicon) is the gate on the 1H27 start.** ③ **rocm.ai productises the reciprocal engineering leg** ("lets developers use today's leading coding agents, including Claude, Codex and Cursor, to create port and optimize code for Instinct"), with AMD "working closely with the leading AI labs, including OpenAI, Anthropic, Meta and others to optimize ROCm for their models" — a confirmation of the BofA 07-24 bullet already on the page, not a replacement for it. ④ ⚠️ **One mild negative logged rather than dropped: Su flagged "lots of customers who are interested in Helios at a more regular scale than gigawatt scale," i.e. AMD expects real non-frontier Helios diversity, which slightly reduces Anthropic's negotiating uniqueness on that platform.** ⚠️ **Source hygiene: machine transcript with garbled product names — normalised silently (MI450-series, ROCm, rocm.ai, EPYC); no garbled token quoted anywhere on the page.** Cross-filed [[AMD]], [[OPENAI]], [[META]].
- **2026-07-30** — **Read-through from the MSFT 4QFY26 earnings call (held 2026-07-29 AMC; Satya Nadella / Amy Hood / Jonathan Neilson (IR)). 2 State-of-play bullets, 2 Full-log rows (07-29 valuation/bull; 07-29 competicao/mixed), 2 Signal-vs-management cells extended (Coding/enterprise leadership; Product/Mythos — verdicts HELD, print reads APPENDED, prior verdicts preserved verbatim), intra-quarter window extended Jul 26 → Jul 29, 1 Sources link, this entry.** ⚠️ **Source-routing correction logged: the auto-router tagged this transcript "UBS" and dated it 2026-07-30 — both wrong. "UBS" came from the analyst roster (Karl Keirstead); it is Microsoft's own call, held 2026-07-29.** **PRIVATE NAME — nothing to supersede: no BBG consensus, no house model, and no rating/PT exists on this page. Reconciled against prior wiki comments only, and NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED — the Series H $965B post-money (28-May-2026) stands as last known valuation.**
  **WHAT IS NET-NEW, AND IT IS A FIRST: Microsoft booked a $3.2 BILLION GAIN on its Anthropic investment in its June quarter** — Hood named it as a discrete item inside a **$0.27 diluted-EPS benefit vs the April guide** (alongside lower-than-expected voluntary-retirement expense, partly offset by severance + Xbox impairment charges), and it is what drove **other income & expense to $2.8 billion "when adjusted for the impact of our investments in OpenAI."** **Every other valuation datapoint on this page is a primary-round or secondary-market print; this is the first time a public, SEC-reporting counterparty has put a number on the appreciation of an Anthropic stake in an audited quarter — and it was roughly the largest single component of a Mag-7 EPS beat.** ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: no implied valuation was inferred, and none is carried anywhere on the page.** Microsoft disclosed **no stake size, no cost basis, no carrying value, no accounting method (fair value vs equity method vs measurement-alternative remeasurement) and no trigger.** The only anchor already on this page for Microsoft's investment size is Nomura's reprise of the Nov-2025 NVDA/MSFT tie-up — **"MSFT up to USD5bn into Anthropic"** (Nomura "Is the cycle over", 2026-06-30) — so the page states only that **the gain is very large relative to Microsoft's disclosed commitment ceiling** and is **directionally consistent with the round progression already on file (Series G $380B post Feb-26 → Series H $965B post May-26, ~2.5x)**. **Two cautions carried explicitly:** (a) **MSFT's fiscal Q4 ran Apr-1 → Jun-30 2026 and therefore straddles the 28-May-2026 Series H close — so the most plausible reading is a round-driven remeasurement marking to a round this page ALREADY KNOWS ABOUT, not evidence of a newer/higher one**; (b) it is a **non-cash mark, not a realisation.** Filed as a **modest counterweight** to the single bearish item on this page (Rothschild/Adley 07-24, unverified TickerTrends "ARR sequential growth has slowed significantly"), with the caveat stated on the page that **a balance-sheet mark and an ARR trajectory are different objects** — the mark most likely reflects the May round rather than June operating trends, so it is not treated as resolving the ARR question.
  **Second item — the Foundry/catalog position and, in the same script, Microsoft's first quantified claim of beating an Anthropic product.** Distribution, sized by the channel owner: **"over 11,000 models"** incl. Anthropic; **"5x increase since the start of the year in the number of customers building with models from multiple providers"** (the successor to the "10,000+ Foundry customers using >1 model, +2x q/q" mark from MSFT IR 2026-05-18 — additive, that mark is not superseded); and **Levi Strauss & Co "using models from OpenAI and Anthropic on Foundry"** across **"more than 1,000 domain-specific agents."** ⚠️ **The competitive counterpart: "In security MAI, Cyber 1 Flash achieves better performance than much larger Mithos [Mythos] model, but at half the cost when combined with our multi-agent security harness," with Nadella adding "50% less cost… because 90% of the tasks are done by the Cyber Flash One model and 10% of the tasks you still go to the frontier" (MDASH data in Cybergym).** **This UPGRADES the existing 07-27 row (The Information framing Project Perception as "a cheaper alternative to Anthropic's Mythos") from press characterisation to a first-party claim with a cost figure and a routing ratio — the 07-27 row is NOT duplicated, the new row cross-references it.** Logged with its limits: the benchmark basis is Microsoft's own and it compares a routed multi-agent SYSTEM against a single larger model, so it is not like-for-like; and Mythos remains unreleased/enterprise-gated by Anthropic's own choice, so a partner is monetising the cyber-agent category first (Project Perception moving beyond private preview on consumption pricing). Read carried on the page: this is a **platform-wide token-cost-reduction programme** (the same claim shape was aimed at OpenAI — MAI Code 1 Flash at GPT-5.6-comparable quality in Excel for less), on which **Anthropic is on the wrong side as a supplier and the right side as a catalog participant** — it keeps the distribution and the 10% that must be frontier. **Not disclosed and left open: any expansion of Anthropic's Azure compute arrangement (up to 1GW / ~$30B per JPM 2026-06-16) and any Claude-token revenue figure through Foundry.** Cross-filed [[MSFT]], [[OPENAI]].
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook + Twitter). 3 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **The commercially important item is The Information's read on Claude Code's competitive position, and the load-bearing fact is a retention result rather than a growth result: “costs for Anthropic's customers have surged with its shift to usage-based pricing,” yet “many enterprises have chosen to stick with Claude Code” — switching did not follow the price increase.** The full picture is genuinely two-sided: **Claude Code's dominance is “proving hard to shake” and it moved companies en masse off [[MSFT]]'s GitHub Copilot and Cursor between end-2025 and early 2026**, but Anthropic now faces **growing competition and explicit customer skepticism over pricing**, a materially stronger **Codex** from [[OPENAI]], and **Chinese open-source models that have grown significantly more capable.** For the revenue thesis, the pricing-power evidence is the most valuable thing in the note. **Separately, and worth tracking as a distinct policy/reputation risk rather than a product one: the open-weights position paper drew the sharpest public backlash this page has recorded.** @DavidSacks (22.4k likes) accuses Anthropic of “breathtaking hypocrisy” for claiming fair use on training over authors' objections while treating training on Claude's outputs as IP theft; @Mayhem4Markets lays out the cited record (**~500,000 copyrighted books from shadow libraries; a federal judge finding the training fair use; a $1.5bn settlement of the piracy allegation; Amodei now naming DeepSeek, Moonshot and MiniMax over “industrial-scale distillation” — 16mn exchanges via 24,000 fake accounts**); @Hesamation argues the real criticism is hostility to frontier open-source development via export controls, oversight and regulation that favour a well-capitalised incumbent. Even the sympathetic read (@natolambert) lands on *“banning distillation is still dumb.”* ⚠️ **All of that is social sentiment, not analysis — logged because regulatory posture and enterprise brand are real inputs, and because engagement here ran an order of magnitude above this page's usual Twitter rows.** Also logged: Jefferies' desk describing a **“MISSION CRITICAL partner to businesses like ANTHROPIC.”** Cross-filed [[OPENAI]], [[MSFT]], [[SKHYNIX]] (the reported Anthropic server-DRAM/eSSD/HBM sourcing thread).

On top of the fixed $30 fee, customers don't see the value**" (Partner 7); one partner priced a real multi-source research task at "**6,500 credits, or $65**" and flags that "**we haven't seen the cost analysis of Copilot Cowork vs Claude Cowork/ChatGPT yet**" — with Customer 4 stating the rule that head-to-head will turn on: "if one's crazy more expensive than the other and they're technologically close, **the commercial side will win**." **Share: a clean positive.** UBS's semi-annual Enterprise AI survey has M365 Copilot retaining mid-large-enterprise leadership "despite **share gains from OpenAI (via ChatGPT Work) and Anthropic Claude (via Claude Cowork)**," with Gemini, Grok and Perplexity losing ground — Anthropic is scored an outright share gainer in knowledge work. Dylan Patel 07-10). **The structural item worth carrying forward:** UBS frames the consensus investor view that "an AI model market dominated by three frontier lab giants (**Anthropic**, OpenAI and Google) creates risk for the hyperscalers, with too much economic power concentrated in too few customers, while a more diffuse and competitive multi-model market diminishes that risk," and reads Satya's Jun-14 / Jul-12 / Jul-24 blog posts as Microsoft positioning explicitly against "the growing control/power of a few frontier AI model providers (Anthropic, OpenAI and Google)." i.e. Anthropic's newest and largest distribution partner is publicly campaigning to commoditize Anthropic's layer — the distribution-vs-disintermediation tension on this page, now stated by a third party rather than inferred. | 2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch) | **5 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded** — Sonnet 5's $3/$15 rate (Barclays 07-17) is confirmed, not replaced. **The single most useful addition is a same-benchmark cost comparison, which is a different and more decision-relevant number than sticker pricing: JPM published the total dollar cost to run the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index — Fable 5 $5,631; Opus 4.8 $3,753; Kimi-K3 $2,710; qwen3.7-plus $311; deepseek-v4-flash $74.** On that basis Fable 5 costs **~2.1x Kimi-K3, ~18x qwen3.7-plus and ~76x deepseek-v4-flash**, because the models burn very different token volumes to reach the same result. Sticker rates for the record (company websites as of 7/21/26): **Fable 5 $10.00/$50.00 per M in/out — the top of the entire market; Opus 4.8 ~$5/$25; gpt-5.6-sol ~$5/$30; Grok 4.5 $2/$6.** Two dated items: **Sonnet 5's introductory $2/$10 runs through 8/31/26 and reverts to $3/$15 on 9/1/26** (so the effective price rises ~50% on Sep 1), and **Opus 5.0 shipped 7/24/26 at $5/$25**, same as Opus 4.8 and excluded from JPM's table for lack of third-party benchmarking. **The counterweight is genuinely strong and lands the same day from a house bearish almost everything else in AI. Redburn's Haissl argues the comparison should be cost-per-task, not sticker: in his OWN Claude Code usage, "stored-cache reads were ~98% of tokens, with fresh input under 2%," and open-weight models "repeat failed tasks… while frontier models don't," so they are "only 2x cheaper when you adjust for cache."** Worth noting rather than adjudicating: **JPM's own Fable-5-vs-Kimi-K3 all-in ratio is ~2.1x, essentially Redburn's "2x"** — so the two houses may agree on the frontier-vs-Kimi gap, and the real dispute is the cheap tail (qwen ~18x, deepseek-v4-flash ~76x). If that reading is right, **Anthropic's exposure is not to Kimi-class models but to the very cheap tier absorbing high-volume simple work** — which is exactly what the federated-routing layers JPM documents (MSFT Foundry Model Router, Intuit GenOS, Zoom's Z-Scorer, Salesforce Agentforce, Cloudflare AI Gateway) are engineered to do. **Second, a finding that cuts against the popular narrative: on Epoch AI's ECI score, JPM finds the performance gap between the top-3 open-weight and top-3 closed-weight models has been "relatively stable over the last couple of years, rather than materially converging."** Cheaper, not catching up. **Third, the distillation dispute converted into an explicit sanctions threat on 07-22, and the timeline is now consolidated on the page: Anthropic's Feb 23 2026 blog naming DeepSeek/Moonshot/Minimax (Minimax 13mn exchanges, Moonshot 3.4mn+ via "hundreds of fraudulent accounts" whose request metadata "matched the public profiles of senior Moonshot staff," later attempting to reconstruct "Claude's reasoning traces"); the June 24 letter calling Alibaba's the "largest known distillation attack on Anthropic to date"; Kimi 3 going viral July 16 and Moonshot pausing subscriptions July 19; then Treasury Secretary Bessent on July 22 — "open source is not open season on American IP… sanctions and Entity List designations will be on the table," plus "we're finding watermarks of our U.S. large language models on many of the Chinese models" — with Kratsios (OSTP) and Helberg (State) alongside.** That is the strongest policy tailwind logged here. **Kept explicitly two-sided: NVDA published an open letter (with MSFT) urging Washington NOT to restrict open-weight models, while Anthropic and [[OPENAI]] are reported to be lobbying FOR restrictions, "increasingly rattled by China's advances" (NYT, 07-25)** — compute layer and model layer now openly opposed, with the administration reportedly leaning to per-model national-security review over a blanket ban. **Fourth, the sensitivity to watch: UBS writes that "investors are now on edge for any update on OpenAI and Anthropic growth,"** and its four-part open-source conclusion puts the equity risk at the frontier labs rather than the hyperscalers or GPUs. Corroborating demand-side behaviour, WSJ (07-26): **"CTOs… utilizing lower-cost, open-source models from China for most tasks and reserving only the most specialized and complex queries for OpenAI and Anthropic"** — down-tiering as observed behaviour, which is compatible with revenue growth *if* the retained queries are the valuable ones. That remains the unresolved question and it is the same one behind the 07-22 UBS and 07-24 Rothschild/TickerTrends bear datapoints. **Fifth, capability evidence: a live Claude Code demo built a portfolio-monitoring dashboard in 10 minutes at BofA's Transforming World Conference**, alongside the (methodology-free, so treat loosely) claim that **AI has cut the cost of a due-diligence report by 99.98% in three years**. Dedup: the "unusual plan for employee stock sales post-IPO" item in this weekend's Information newsletter was already filed on 07-24 (mandatory 10b5-1 plans pre-IPO) and was not re-added; the **[[GOOG]] $44bn data-centre lease-guarantee disclosure — incurred, per The Information, to "supply Anthropic and others" with TPUs — is filed on GOOG's page** and is the contingent-liability channel behind the same customer-concentration question. Cross-filed [[OPENAI]], [[NVDA]], [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]], [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). |
